#include <bits/stdc++.h>
using namespace std;

typedef long long ll;

vector<vector<int>> adj;
vector<int> a, parent, depth;
vector<vector<int>> up;
int LOG;

void dfs(int u, int p) {
    parent[u] = p;
    depth[u] = depth[p] + 1;
    for (int v : adj[u]) {
        if (v != p) {
            dfs(v, u);
        }
    }
}

void preprocess_lca(int n, int root) {
    LOG = 0;
    while ((1 << LOG) <= n) LOG++;
    up.assign(LOG, vector<int>(n + 1));
    parent[root] = 0;
    depth[root] = -1;
    dfs(root, 0);
    up[0] = parent;
    for (int j = 1; j < LOG; j++) {
        for (int i = 1; i <= n; i++) {
            up[j][i] = up[j - 1][up[j - 1][i]];
        }
    }
}

int lca(int u, int v) {
    if (depth[u] < depth[v]) swap(u, v);
    int k = depth[u] - depth[v];
    for (int j = 0; j < LOG; j++) {
        if (k & (1 << j)) {
            u = up[j][u];
        }
    }
    if (u == v) return u;
    for (int j = LOG - 1; j >= 0; j--) {
        if (up[j][u] != up[j][v]) {
            u = up[j][u];
            v = up[j][v];
        }
    }
    return parent[u];
}

int distance(int u, int v) {
    return depth[u] + depth[v] - 2 * depth[lca(u, v)];
}

int main() {
    ios_base::sync_with_stdio(false);
    cin.tie(nullptr);

    int n;
    cin >> n;
    a.resize(n + 1);
    adj.resize(n + 1);
    parent.resize(n + 1);
    depth.resize(n + 1);

    int root = 1;
    for (int i = 1; i <= n; i++) {
        cin >> a[i];
        if (a[i] < a[root]) {
            root = i;
        }
    }

    for (int i = 0; i < n - 1; i++) {
        int u, v;
        cin >> u >> v;
        adj[u].push_back(v);
        adj[v].push_back(u);
    }

    preprocess_lca(n, root);

    vector<int> nodes;
    for (int i = 1; i <= n; i++) {
        if (i != root) {
            nodes.push_back(i);
        }
    }
    sort(nodes.begin(), nodes.end(), [&](int x, int y) { return a[x] < a[y]; });

    vector<ll> best(n + 1, LLONG_MAX);
    vector<int> best_node(n + 1, -1);

    for (int u : nodes) {
        int d = distance(u, root);
        int log_d = d == 0 ? 0 : 31 - __builtin_clz(d);
        if (d > (1 << log_d)) log_d++;
        ll cost = (ll)a[root] * (1 + log_d);
        best[u] = cost;
        best_node[u] = root;
    }

    ll total = 0;
    for (int u : nodes) {
        if (best_node[u] == -1) {
            continue;
        }
        int p = best_node[u];
        int d = distance(u, p);
        int log_d = d == 0 ? 0 : 31 - __builtin_clz(d);
        if (d > (1 << log_d)) log_d++;
        total += a[u] + a[p] + (ll)a[p] * log_d;
    }

    cout << total << "\n";

    return 0;

The user can choose when to stop. But the idea is, the optimal strategy would involve choosing a stopping time when we can maximize the expectation of the net profit.

Hmm, that's the crux here. Because the optimal strategy might be to stop when you have a profit (so you can avoid getting a worse net profit through the refund), or sometimes to continue even with a loss, if you can have a chance to turn things around.

This feels like an optimization problem where we need to model the process as a Markov decision process. At each step, decide to stop (and calculate the expected value) or continue, and choose the bets accordingly.

Wait, perhaps it's better to think of the trading between stopping and betting further.

Let me consider the stopping decision at any moment. Suppose I have played some number of bets and have a current profit of P (could be positive or negative). At this point, I have the option to stop, leading to an expected value of:

   E_stop = net_profit(P) = max(P, (1 - x/100)*P)

Alternatively, I can place another bet, which gives me a 50-50 (or p versus (1-p)) chance for some outcome, leading to a new state, and then continue making decisions.

So the value function V(P) is the maximum expected value achievable from the current state.

So V(P) = max [ E_stop , E_continue ]

where E_continue is (prob_win)*V(P + 1) + (prob_lose)*V(P -1)

Wait, because if you bet and win, the profit becomes P + 1. If you lose, it's P -1.

So the recursive equation is:

V(P) = max [ E_stop, (p * V(P +1)) + ( (1-p) * V(P -1) ) ]

But where do I start? The problem allows me to choose when to start and stop. The initial state is P=0 (since you start at 0 profit) with 0 bets made. We are required to maximize the expectation from choosing actions here.

Wait but in the problem's context, the user is allowed to choose the strategy, including the number of bets. So, starting at 0, choose to bet or stop.

Wait, the initial profit is zero. If I choose to stop immediately, the profit is zero (since P is zero, so net profit is zero). Alternatively, I can bet once: after that, profit may be +1 or -1, then proceed further. So the idea is to choose when to stop to maximize the expectation.

Therefore, we need to model this as a DP where for each possible profit P (positive or negative), we can compute the maximum value V(P), which depends recursively on the next states.

However, the problem is that the profit can, in principle, be any integer (since each bet changes it by +1 or -1). So the state space is unbounded, but perhaps there's a stopping condition where for certain P's we can decide to stop.

Alternatively, maybe we can model it as an infinite state problem but find a pattern or mathematical formula.

#include <vector>
#include <algorithm>
#include <queue>
#include <climits>
#include <cstdio>

using namespace std;

struct Edge {
    int to, rev, cap;
    long long cost;
    Edge(int to, int rev, int cap, long long cost) : to(to), rev(rev), cap(cap), cost(cost) {}
};

struct Task {
    int s, t, c, end, original_index;
    Task(int s, int t, int c, int original_index) : s(s), t(t), c(c), original_index(original_index) {
        end = s + t - 1;
    }
};

vector<vector<Edge>> g;
vector<long long> potential, dist;
vector<int> parent, parent_edge;

void add_edge(int from, int to, int cap, long long cost) {
    g[from].emplace_back(to, g[to].size(), cap, cost);
    g[to].emplace_back(from, g[from].size()-1, 0, -cost);
}

pair<long long, int> min_cost_flow(int source, int sink, int max_flow) {
    long long total_cost = 0;
    int flow = 0;
    potential.assign(g.size(), LLONG_MAX);
    parent.assign(g.size(), -1);
    parent_edge.assign(g.size(), -1);
    vector<bool> in_queue(g.size(), false);
    queue<int> q;

    // Bellman-Ford for initial potentials
    potential[source] = 0;
    q.push(source);
    in_queue[source] = true;
    while (!q.empty()) {
        int u = q.front(); q.pop();
        in_queue[u] = false;
        for (const Edge& e : g[u]) {
            if (e.cap > 0 && potential[e.to] > potential[u] + e.cost) {
                potential[e.to] = potential[u] + e.cost;
                if (!in_queue[e.to]) {
                    q.push(e.to);
                    in_queue[e.to] = true;
                }
            }
        }
    }

    while (flow < max_flow) {
        dist.assign(g.size(), LLONG_MAX);
        parent.assign(g.size(), -1);
        parent_edge.assign(g.size(), -1);
        using P = pair<long long, int>;
        priority_queue<P, vector<P>, greater<P>> pq;
        dist[source] = 0;
        pq.emplace(0, source);
        while (!pq.empty()) {
            auto [d, u] = pq.top(); pq.pop();
            if (d > dist[u]) continue;
            for (size_t i = 0; i < g[u].size(); ++i) {
                const Edge& e = g[u][i];
                long long new_dist = d + e.cost + potential[u] - potential[e.to];
                if (e.cap > 0 && new_dist < dist[e.to]) {
                    dist[e.to] = new_dist;
                    parent[e.to] = u;
                    parent_edge[e.to] = i;
                    pq.emplace(new_dist, e.to);
                }
            }
        }
        if (dist[sink] == LLONG_MAX) break;

        for (size_t u = 0; u < g.size(); ++u)
            if (dist[u] < LLONG_MAX)
                potential[u] += dist[u];

        int augment = max_flow - flow;
        for (int u = sink; u != source; u = parent[u]) {
            int p = parent[u];
            Edge& e = g[p][parent_edge[u]];
            augment = min(augment, e.cap);
        }
        for (int u = sink; u != source; u = parent[u]) {
            int p = parent[u];
            Edge& e = g[p][parent_edge[u]];
            e.cap -= augment;
            g[e.to][e.rev].cap += augment;
        }
        flow += augment;
        total_cost += (long long)augment * (potential[sink] - (parent[sink] == -1 ? 0 : potential[source]));
    }
    return {total_cost, flow};
}

int main() {
    int n, k;
    scanf("%d %d", &n, &k);
    vector<Task> tasks;
    for (int i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
        int s, t, c;
        scanf("%d %d %d", &s, &t, &c);
        tasks.emplace_back(s, t, c, i);
    }

    sort(tasks.begin(), tasks.end(), [](const Task& a, const Task& b) {
        return a.end < b.end;
    });

    int num_nodes = 2 * n + 2;
    int source = 0;
    int sink = 1;
    g.resize(num_nodes);

    for (int i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
        int in_node = 2 + 2 * i;
        int out_node = in_node + 1;
        add_edge(in_node, out_node, 1, -tasks[i].c);
        add_edge(source, in_node, 1, 0);
        add_edge(out_node, sink, 1, 0);
    }

    for (int i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
        int out_i = 2 + 2 * i + 1;
        int s_i = tasks[i].s;
        int t_i = tasks[i].t;
        int end_i = s_i + t_i;
        for (int j = i + 1; j < n; ++j) {
            if (tasks[j].s >= end_i) {
                int in_j = 2 + 2 * j;
                add_edge(out_i, in_j, 1, 0);
            }
        }
    }

    auto [total_cost, flow] = min_cost_flow(source, sink, k);

    vector<int> result(n, 0);
    for (int i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
        int in_node = 2 + 2 * i;
        for (const Edge& e : g[in_node]) {
            if (e.to == in_node + 1 && e.cap == 0) {
                result[tasks[i].original_index] = 1;
                break;
            }
        }
    }

    for (int i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
        printf("%d%c", result[i], " \n"[i == n-1]);
    }

    return 0;

</think>To determine the number of real solutions to the equation \(\lfloor \sin x \rfloor = \left\{\frac{x}{2\pi}\right\} + \left\{\frac{2x}{5\pi}\right\}\), we need to analyze the possible values of both sides of the equation.

1. **Left Side Analysis:**
   - The floor function \(\lfloor \sin x \rfloor\) can only take the values \(-1\), \(0\), or \(1\), since \(\sin x\) ranges between \(-1\) and \(1\).
   - \(\lfloor \sin x \rfloor = -1\) when \(\sin x \in [-1, 0)\).
   - \(\lfloor \sin x \rfloor = 0\) when \(\sin x \in [0, 1)\).
   - \(\lfloor \sin x \rfloor = 1\) only when \(\sin x = 1\), which occurs at isolated points \(x = \frac{\pi}{2} + 2k\pi\) for integers \(k\).

2. **Right Side Analysis:**
   - The right-hand side is the sum of two fractional parts: \(\left\{\frac{x}{2\pi}\right\} + \left\{\frac{2x}{5\pi}\right\}\).
   - Each fractional part is between \(0\) (inclusive) and \(1\) (exclusive), so their sum is between \(0\) and \(2\) (exclusive).
   - Therefore, the right-hand side can take any value in \([0, 2)\).

3. **Equation Constraints:**
   - Since \(\lfloor \sin x \rfloor\) must be an integer, the right-hand side must also be an integer between \(0\) and \(2\).
   - This restricts the possible values of the right-hand side to \(0\) or \(1\).

   - **Case 1: \(\lfloor \sin x \rfloor = 0\)**:

#include <iostream>
#include <vector>
#include <string>
#include <unordered_set>
using namespace std;

vector<pair<string, string>> get_candidates(const string& w, const string& wp) {
    vector<pair<string, string>> res;
    int n = w.size();
    if (w == wp) return res;

    int a = -1, b = -1;
    for (int i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
        if (w[i] != wp[i]) {
            if (a == -1) a = i;
            b = i;
        }
    }

    for (int j = 0; j <= a; ++j) {
        bool prefix_ok = true;
        for (int k = 0; k < j; ++k) {
            if (w[k] != wp[k]) {
                prefix_ok = false;
                break;
            }
        }
        if (!prefix_ok) continue;

        int L = b - j + 1;
        if (j + L > n) continue;

        bool suffix_ok = true;
        for (int k = j + L; k < n; ++k) {
            if (w[k] != wp[k]) {
                suffix_ok = false;
                break;
            }
        }
        if (!suffix_ok) continue;

        string s = w.substr(j, L);
        string t = wp.substr(j, L);

        bool exists = false;
        for (int pos = 0; pos + L <= j; ++pos) {
            if (w.substr(pos, L) == s) {
                exists = true;
                break;
            }
        }
        if (!exists) {
            res.emplace_back(s, t);
        }
    }

    return res;
}

int main() {
    ios::sync_with_stdio(false);
    cin.tie(nullptr);

    int n;
    cin >> n;

    vector<string> w(n), wp(n);
    for (int i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
        cin >> w[i];
    }
    for (int i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
        cin >> wp[i];
    }

    vector<int> changing;
    for (int i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
        if (w[i] != wp[i]) {
            changing.push_back(i);
        }
    }

    if (changing.empty()) {
        cout << "NO\n";
        return 0;
    }

    vector<pair<string, string>> common;
    bool first = true;

    for (int i : changing) {
        auto candidates = get_candidates(w[i], wp[i]);
        if (candidates.empty()) {
            cout << "NO\n";
            return 0;
        }
        if (first) {
            common = candidates;
            first = false;
        } else {
            unordered_set<string> seen;
            for (auto& p : common) {
                seen.insert(p.first + "\0" + p.second);
            }
            vector<pair<string, string>> temp;
            for (auto& p : candidates) {
                string key = p.first + "\0" + p.second;
                if (seen.count(key)) {
                    temp.push_back(p);
                }
            }
            common = temp;
            if (common.empty()) {
                cout << "NO\n";
                return 0;
            }
        }
    }

    for (auto& [s, t] : common) {
        bool valid = true;
        for (int i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
            if (w[i] == wp[i]) {
                if (w[i].find(s) != string::npos) {
                    valid = false;
                    break;
                }
            }
        }
        if (valid) {
            cout << "YES\n";
            cout << s << '\n' << t << '\n';
            return 0;
        }
    }

    cout << "NO\n";
    return 0;
}<#sep#>\) (exclusive).
   - Therefore, the right-hand side can take any value in \([0, 2)\).

3. **Equation Constraints:**
   - Since \(\lfloor \sin x \rfloor\) must be an integer, the right-hand side must also be an integer between \(0\) and \(2\).
   - This restricts the possible values of the right-hand side to \(0\) or \(1\).

4. **Case Analysis:**
   - **Case 1: \(\lfloor \sin x \rfloor = 0\)**:
     - This requires \(\left\{\frac{x}{2\pi}\right\} + \left\{\frac{2x}{5\pi}\right\} = 0\).
     - Both fractional parts must be zero simultaneously, leading to:
       - \(\frac{x}{2\pi}\) being an integer, so \(x = 2\pi k\).
       - \(\frac{2x}{5\pi}\) being an integer, so \(x = \frac{5\pi}{2} m\).
     - The common solutions occur when \(x\) is a multiple of the least common multiple (LCM) of \(2\pi\) and \(\frac{5\pi}{2}\), which is \(10\pi\). Thus, \(x = 10\pi n\) for integers \(n\).
   - **Case 2: \(\lfloor \sin x \rfloor = 1\)**:
     - This would require \(\left\{\frac{x}{2\pi}\right\} + \left\{\frac{2x}{5\pi}\right\} = 1\), but \(\sin x = 1\) only at isolated points where the right-hand side does not equal \(1\), making this case impossible.

5. **Conclusion:**
   - The only valid solutions are when \(\lfloor \sin x \rfloor = 0\) and the right-hand side equals \(0\), which occurs at \(x = 10\pi n\) for all integers \(n\).
   - These solutions are spaced at intervals of \(10\pi\), leading to infinitely many real solutions.

Thus, the number of real solutions is \(\boxed{\infty}\).<#sep#>-p)(P-1)

#include <iostream>
#include <vector>
#include <algorithm>
#include <cmath>
#include <iomanip>

using namespace std;

struct Point {
    double x, y;
    bool operator<(const Point &p) const {
        if (x != p.x) return x < p.x;
        return y < p.y;
    }
    bool operator==(const Point &p) const {
        return (fabs(x - p.x) < 1e-8 && fabs(y - p.y) < 1e-8);
    }
};

double cross(const Point &a, const Point &b, const Point &c) {
    return (b.x - a.x) * (c.y - a.y) - (b.y - a.y) * (c.x - a.x);
}

vector<Point> convex_hull(vector<Point> pts) {
    int n = pts.size();
    if (n <= 1) return pts;

    sort(pts.begin(), pts.end());
    pts.erase(unique(pts.begin(), pts.end()), pts.end());
    n = pts.size();
    if (n <= 1) return pts;

    vector<Point> hull;
    int k = 0;
    for (int i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
        while (k >= 2 && cross(hull[k-2], hull[k-1], pts[i]) <= 1e-8) {
            hull.pop_back();
            --k;
        }
        hull.push_back(pts[i]);
        ++k;
    }

    int upper_hull_start = k + 1;
    for (int i = n-2; i >= 0; --i) {
        while (k >= upper_hull_start && cross(hull[k-2], hull[k-1], pts[i]) <= 1e-8) {
            hull.pop_back();
            --k;
        }
        hull.push_back(pts[i]);
        ++k;
    }

    hull.pop_back();
    return hull;
}

double compute_area(const vector<Point> &hull) {
    double area = 0.0;
    int n = hull.size();
    for (int i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
        int j = (i + 1) % n;
        area += (hull[i].x * hull[j].y - hull[j].x * hull[i].y);
    }
    return fabs(area) / 2.0;
}

int main() {
    int n;
    cin >> n;
    vector<Point> points(n);
    for (int i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
        cin >> points[i].x >> points[i].y;
    }

    double max_area = -1.0;
    double best_theta = 0.0;

    const double step = 0.001;
    const int steps = ceil(2 * M_PI / step);

    for (int i = 0; i < steps; ++i) {
        double theta = i * step;
        vector<Point> rotated;
        rotated.reserve(n);
        for (auto &p : points) {
            double x_new = p.x * cos(theta) - p.y * sin(theta);
            double y_new = p.x * sin(theta) + p.y * cos(theta);
            rotated.push_back({x_new, y_new});
        }

        vector<Point> hull = convex_hull(rotated);
        if (hull.size() < 3) continue;

        double area = compute_area(hull);
        if (area > max_area + 1e-8) {
            max_area = area;
            best_theta = theta;
        } else if (fabs(area - max_area) < 1e-8 && theta < best_theta) {
            best_theta = theta;
        }
    }

    cout << fixed << setprecision(3) << best_theta << endl;

    return 0;

+## @param topologySpreadConstraints<#sep#># 2          SHA1和MD5的算法说明

SHA1和MD5的算法都是从MD4算法改进而来的2种算法，基本思路都是将信息分成N个分组，每组64个字节，每个分组都进行摘要运算。当一个分组的摘要运算完毕后，将上一个分组的结果也用于下一个分组的运算。

MD5最后生成的摘要信息是16个字节，SHA1是20个字节。

MD5和SHA1的分组信息运算，分组里面的的数据都会被视为16个DWORD，而MD5算法认为这些DWORD的字节序列是LITTLE-ENDIAN,而SHA1的算法认为DWORD是BIG-ENDIAN的。所以在不同字节序的主机上要进行转换。

15 #ifndef ZEN_SWAP_UINT16
16 #define ZEN_SWAP_UINT16(x)  ((((x) & 0xff00) >>  8) | (((x) & 0x00ff) <<  8))
17 #endif
18 #ifndef ZEN_SWAP_UINT32
19 #define ZEN_SWAP_UINT32(x)  ((((x) & 0xff000000) >> 24) | (((x) & 0x00ff0000) >>  8) | \
20     (((x) & 0x0000ff00) <<  8) | (((x) & 0x000000ff) << 24))
21 #endif
22 #ifndef ZEN_SWAP_UINT64
23 #define ZEN_SWAP_UINT64(x)  ((((x) & 0xff00000000000000) >> 56) | (((x) & 0x00ff000000000000) >>  40) | \
24     (((x) & 0x0000ff0000000000) >> 24) | (((x) & 0x000000ff00000000) >>  8) | \
25     (((x) & 0x00000000ff000000) << 8 ) | (((x) & 0x0000000000ff0000) <<  24) | \
26     (((x) & 0x000000000000ff00) << 40 ) | (((x) & 0x00000000000000ff) <<  56))
27 #endif

29 //将一个（字符串）数组，拷贝到另外一个uint32_t数组，同时每个uint32_t反字节序
30 void *swap_uint32_memcpy(void *to, const void *from, size_t length)
31 {
32     memcpy(to, from, length);
33     size_t remain_len =  (4 - (length & 3)) & 3;
34
35     //数据不是4字节的倍数,补充0
36     if (remain_len)
37     {
38         for (size_t i = 0; i < remain_len; ++i)
39         {
40             *((char *)(to) + length + i) = 0;
41         }
42         //调整成4的倍数
43         length += remain_len;
44     }
45
46     //所有的数据反转
47     for (size_t i = 0; i < length / 4; ++i)
48     {
49         ((uint32_t *)to)[i] = ZEN_SWAP_UINT32(((uint32_t *)to)[i]);
50     }
51
53 }

110 #define ROTR32(dword, n) ((dword) >> (n) ^ ((dword) << (32 - (n))))

#include <bits/stdc++.h>
using namespace std;

struct Point {
    int x, y;
    int index;
    bool is_ship;
};

vector<pair<int, int>> solve(vector<Point> ships, vector<Point> bases) {
    int n = ships.size();
    if (n == 1) {
        return {{ships[0].index, bases[0].index}};
    }

    random_device rd;
    mt19937 gen(rd());
    uniform_int_distribution<int> distrib(-1e9, 1e9);

    while (true) {
        int dx = distrib(gen);
        int dy = distrib(gen);
        while (dx == 0 && dy == 0) {
            dx = distrib(gen);
            dy = distrib(gen);
        }

        vector<Point> all;
        for (auto s : ships) all.push_back(s);
        for (auto b : bases) all.push_back(b);

        sort(all.begin(), all.end(), [dx, dy](const Point& a, const Point& b) {
            long long proj_a = (long long)dx * a.x + (long long)dy * a.y;
            long long proj_b = (long long)dx * b.x + (long long)dy * b.y;
            return proj_a < proj_b;
        });

        int m = n / 2;
        int current_ships = 0, current_bases = 0;
        for (size_t i = 0; i < all.size(); ++i) {
            if (all[i].is_ship) current_ships++;
            else current_bases++;

            if (current_ships == m && current_bases == m) {
                vector<Point> left_ships, left_bases, right_ships, right_bases;
                for (size_t j = 0; j <= i; ++j) {
                    if (all[j].is_ship) left_ships.push_back(all[j]);
                    else left_bases.push_back(all[j]);
                }
                for (size_t j = i + 1; j < all.size(); ++j) {
                    if (all[j].is_ship) right_ships.push_back(all[j]);
                    else right_bases.push_back(all[j]);
                }

                auto left = solve(left_ships, left_bases);
                auto right = solve(right_ships, right_bases);
                left.insert(left.end(), right.begin(), right.end());
                return left;
            }
        }
    }
}

int main() {
    ios::sync_with_stdio(false);
    cin.tie(nullptr);

    int n;
    cin >> n;

    vector<Point> ships(n);
    for (int i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
        cin >> ships[i].x >> ships[i].y;
        ships[i].index = i;
        ships[i].is_ship = true;
    }

    vector<Point> bases(n);
    for (int i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
        cin >> bases[i].x >> bases[i].y;
        bases[i].index = i + 1;
        bases[i].is_ship = false;
    }

    auto result = solve(ships, bases);
    vector<int> output(n);
    for (auto& p : result) {
        output[p.first] = p.second;
    }

    for (int i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
        cout << output[i] << '\n';
    }

    return 0;
}<#sep#> of pairs of the number of functions where those two pairs are mutual, and so on.

Therefore, applying inclusion-exclusion, the number of functions with no mutual pairs is:

Total functions - sum_{pairs} functions_with_pair + sum_{two pairs} functions_with_both_pairs - ... + (-1)^k sum_{k pairs} functions_with_k_pairs ... etc.

So, in formula:

N = 6^7 - C(7,2)*4*6^{7 - 2} + C(7,2)C(5,2)/2! *4^2*6^{7 - 4} - ... + (-1)^k C(7,2)C(5,2)...C(7 - 2(k-1),2)/k! *4^k*6^{7 - 2k} + ... 

Wait, this is getting a bit complicated, but maybe let's formalize it.

Each mutual pair involves two people, say i and j. For each such pair, if we fix that i and j throw at each other, then the remaining 5 people can throw at anyone except themselves, but with the caveat that we might have to consider overlapping pairs. Wait, but inclusion-exclusion needs to account for overlapping pairs.

But actually, when we use inclusion-exclusion, each term in the inclusion-exclusion corresponds to the number of functions that have at least a certain set of mutual pairs. However, mutual pairs are independent if they don't share a person. If they do share a person, then they interfere with each other.

But perhaps this is getting too complex. Let me check the standard approach for this problem.

Wait, actually, this problem is similar to the concept of "derangements with no 2-cycles." In permutations, derangements are permutations with no fixed points. If we also exclude 2-cycles, then it's a derangement with no 2-cycles. However, in our case, the functions are not permutations, so it's different.

Alternatively, maybe it's easier to model the problem as follows:

Each person chooses a target, not themselves. We need the number of such choices where no two people choose each other.

Alternatively, if we model the problem as a directed graph, each vertex has out-degree 1, and we want the number of such graphs with no 2-cycles.

So the problem is similar to counting the number of such graphs. The total number is 6^7. The number of graphs with no mutual edges is what we need.

This seems related to the concept of "oriented graphs" where there are no mutual edges, but in our case, each vertex has exactly one outgoing edge.

Alternatively, maybe we can model this as follows:

Each person can choose any of the 6 others. However, if person A chooses person B, then person B cannot choose person A. So perhaps we can model this as follows:

For each pair (A,B), the number of functions where A and B choose each other is 1*1*6^{5} (since A must choose B, B must choose A, and the other 5 people can choose freely). However, but we have to consider overlapping pairs.

But inclusion-exclusion is the way to go here.

Let’s denote by S the total number of functions: 6^7.

Let’s let A_{i,j} be the set of functions where person i and person j throw at each other. Then, the number we want is S - |∪A_{i,j}|, which by inclusion-exclusion is:

S - ∑|A_{i,j}| + ∑|A_{i,j} ∩ A_{k,l}| - ∑|A_{i,j} ∩ A_{k,l} ∩ A_{m,n}|} + ... + (-1)^m |A_{i,j} ∩ ... ∩ A_{p,q}|}.

So first, the number of A_{i,j} sets. Each pair (i,j) where i ≠ j. How many such pairs? C(7,2) = 21.

Each A_{i,j} corresponds to functions where i→j and j→i. The remaining 5 people can throw at anyone except themselves. So the number of such functions is 1 (for i→j) *1 (for j→i) *6^5 (for the remaining 5). Therefore, |A_{i,j}| = 6^5.

Therefore, the first term is 21*6^5.

Then, for the second term, we have intersections of two A_{i,j} and A_{k,l}. However, we need to consider whether the pairs (i,j) and (k,l) are disjoint or overlapping. If they are disjoint, then the intersection is |A_{i,j} ∩ A_{k,l}| = 1*1*1*1*6^{5 - 2*2 + ...} Wait, no. Let's think.

If we have two disjoint pairs, say (i,j) and (k,l), then in the intersection, i→j, j→i, k→l, l→k. The remaining 7 - 4 = 3 people can each choose any of 6. Wait, no. Each person can choose any of 6, except themselves. But in the mutual pairs, the people in the pairs are choosing someone else. So for the two mutual pairs, i, j, k, l are already assigned, each to their respective partners. The remaining three people can each choose any of 6 (since they can't choose themselves, but they can choose anyone else). So for each of the remaining three, 6 choices. So total for two disjoint pairs: 1*1*1*1*6^3. But how many such pairs of disjoint pairs are there?

First, the number of ways to choose two disjoint pairs: C(7,2)*C(5,2)/2! Because first choose two pairs, but since the order of the pairs doesn't matter, divide by 2!.

So for two disjoint pairs, the number of such terms is C(7,2)*C(5,2)/2! = (21 * 10)/2 = 105. Each contributes 6^3.

However, if the two pairs share a person, like (i,j) and (i,k), then it's impossible for both to happen, because person i cannot both throw to j and k. So such overlapping pairs cannot coexist. Therefore, the intersection of A_{i,j} and A_{i,k} is empty. Therefore, only disjoint pairs contribute to the second term.

Therefore, the second term is + C(7,2)*C(5,2)/2! *6^{7 - 4} = 105 *6^3.

Wait, but wait. Let me check. For two disjoint pairs, the total number of fixed assignments is 4 people assigned in mutual pairs, and the remaining 3 people can choose anyone except themselves. Each of the remaining three has 6 choices (since they can choose any of the other 6 people). So yes, 6^3. So the term is 105 *6^3.

Then, the third term is the intersections of three mutual pairs. Similarly, these have to be three disjoint pairs. The number of ways to choose three disjoint pairs from seven people. But seven is an odd number, so you can't have three disjoint pairs; you need six people. Wait, 3 pairs would cover 6 people, leaving one person remaining. Therefore, you can't have three disjoint pairs in seven people. Wait, but seven people. So if you have three pairs, that's six people, leaving one person. The expectation is the expected net profit from this strategy.

Each time there is a chance to win and stop, or to lose and continue.

Let me model this as following:

Let the expected net profit starting from P=0 following this strategy is E.

To compute E:

At state P=0, we choose to place a bet (since stopping gives 0 and maybe continuation may give better?):

After bet:

- You win with probability p: profit becomes 1, so you stop, getting 1.

- Lose with probability (1-p): profit goes to -1.

 Then, from profit -1, continue.

Thus, the next steps are:

At -1 profit, you can choose to stop, getting (1-x/100)*(-1)= -r (r= (1-x/100)), or continue betting again (and repeat the process).

Wait, but following the strategy of "try to get to +1 and then stop".

 Wait, no, in this strategy, you only stop when P is positive. So at -1:

 you must bet again.

Let me see the expectation:

 Let E_0 is the expected value starting from P=0 with this strategy (bet until +1 then stop).

E_0 = p *1 + (1-p)* [ expected continuing from P=-1 ]

Let me denote E_{-1} as the expected value from P=-1 following this strategy.

Then,

 E_0 = p *1 + (1-p)*E_{-1}

Then, for P=-1:

E_{-1} = p * E_0 + (1-p)* E_{-2} 

Similarly, for P=-2:

Continuing indefinitely, this forms an infinite sequence.

E_{-n} = p * E_{-(n-1)} + (1-p)*E_{-(n+1)} }

This recurrence relation may have solutions.

Let me consider that the sequence must stabilize in some way? Or maybe it can be defined as a linear combination.

Let me define:

E_{-n} = c * r^{n} 

where r is a constant to be found. Let's try this ansatz (hypothetical solution).

Plugging into the recurrence:

E_{-n} = p * E_{-(n-1)} + (1-p)* E_{-(n+1)} 

 => c r^{n} = p c r^{n-1} + (1-p) c r^{n+1}

Divide both sides by c r^{n-1} (assuming c !=0 and r0≠0):

 r = p*1 + (1-p) r^2 

 => r^2 (1-p) - r + p =0 

This quadratic equation:

 (1-p)r² - (1)r + p =0 

Solutions: r= [1 ± sqrt(1-4*(1-p)*p) ] / [2*(1-p)] 

Wait compute discriminant D: 1 -4*(1-p)*p 

= 1-4p +4p² 

Wait 4*(1-p)*p =4p -4p² 

So D is 1- (4p-4p²) = 1-4p +4p² = (1- 2p)^2. 

So sqrt(D) = 1-2p.

Thus,

r = [1 ± (1-2p) ] / (2*(1-p)). 

Taking first solution with "+" sign:

 numerator = 1 +1-2p → 2-2p → 2(1-p)

 → denominator 2(1-p) → ratio is 1 → r=1.

Second solution:

 numerator 1 - (1-2p) = 2p → ratio → 2p/(2(1-p))= p/(1-p)

 So the two roots are r=1 and r = p/(1-p).

Thus, for convergence, perhaps one of these is useful.

So, assuming E_{-n} is a linear combination:

 E_{-n} = A *1^{n} + B (p/(1-p))^n 

Now, given that our process is for the expectation to be convergent, likely the term with ratio less than 1 will dominate. Because p/(1-p) <1? Since p is less than 0.5, so yes:

If p < (1-p) since p <0.5, so p/(1-p) <1, so the term p/(1-p)^n tends to zero as n increases.

Hence, for n approaching infinity, E_{-∞} = A.

To find A and B, we need to apply boundary conditions.

We must determine the value when we get pushed to a very large negative P (-infty), but perhaps physically the game can continue indefinitely.

115 /*!
116 @brief      内部函数，初始化MD5的context，内容
117 @param      ctx
118 */
119 static void zen_md5_init(md5_ctx *ctx)
120 {
121     ctx->length_ = 0;
122     ctx->unprocessed_ = 0;
123
124     /* initialize state */
125     ctx->hash_[0] = 0x67452301;
126     ctx->hash_[1] = 0xefcdab89;
128     ctx->hash_[3] = 0x10325476;
129 }
130
131 /* First, define four auxiliary functions that each take as input
132  * three 32-bit words and returns a 32-bit word.*/
133
134 /* F(x,y,z) = ((y XOR z) AND x) XOR z - is faster then original version */
135 #define MD5_F(x, y, z) ((((y) ^ (z)) & (x)) ^ (z))
136 #define MD5_G(x, y, z) (((x) & (z)) | ((y) & (~z)))
137 #define MD5_H(x, y, z) ((x) ^ (y) ^ (z))
138 #define MD5_I(x, y, z) ((y) ^ ((x) | (~z)))
139
140 /* transformations for rounds 1, 2, 3, and 4. */
141 #define MD5_ROUND1(a, b, c, d, x, s, ac) { \
142         (a) += MD5_F((b), (c), (d)) + (x) + (ac); \
143         (a) = ROTL32((a), (s)); \
144         (a) += (b); \
145     }
146 #define MD5_ROUND2(a, b, c, d, x, s, ac) { \
147         (a) += MD5_G((b), (c), (d)) + (x) + (ac); \
148         (a) = ROTL32((a), (s)); \
149         (a) += (b); \
150     }
151 #define MD5_ROUND3(a, b, c, d, x, s, ac) { \
152         (a) += MD5_H((b), (c), (d)) + (x) + (ac); \
153         (a) = ROTL32((a), (s)); \
154         (a) += (b); \
155     }
156 #define MD5_ROUND4(a, b, c, d, x, s, ac) { \
157         (a) += MD5_I((b), (c), (d)) + (x) + (ac); \
158         (a) = ROTL32((a), (s)); \
159         (a) += (b); \
160     }
161
162
163 /*!
164 @brief      内部函数，将64个字节，16个uint32_t的数组进行摘要（杂凑）处理，处理的数据自己序是小头数据
165 @param      state 存放处理的hash数据结果
166 @param      block 要处理的block，64个字节，16个uint32_t的数组
167 */
168 static void zen_md5_process_block(uint32_t state[4], const uint32_t block[ZEN_MD5_BLOCK_SIZE / 4])
169 {
170     register unsigned a, b, c, d;

183     swap_uint32_memcpy(swap_block, block, 64);
184     x = swap_block;
185 #endif

#include <bits/stdc++.h>
using namespace std;

const int MAXN = 5e5 + 5;

struct Node {
    char c;
    int depth, mask, size, heavy_child;
    vector<int> children;
};

int n;
Node nodes[MAXN];
vector<int> max_subtree;

void compute_masks_and_depths() {
    nodes[1].depth = 0;
    nodes[1].mask = 0;
    queue<int> q;
    q.push(1);
    while (!q.empty()) {
        int u = q.front();
        q.pop();
        for (int v : nodes[u].children) {
            nodes[v].depth = nodes[u].depth + 1;
            nodes[v].mask = nodes[u].mask ^ (1 << (nodes[v].c - 'a'));
            q.push(v);
        }
    }
}

void dfs_size(int u) {
    nodes[u].size = 1;
    nodes[u].heavy_child = -1;
    int max_size = 0;
    for (int v : nodes[u].children) {
        dfs_size(v);
        nodes[u].size += nodes[v].size;
        if (nodes[v].size > max_size) {
            max_size = nodes[v].size;
            nodes[u].heavy_child = v;
        }
    }
}

void dfs(int u, unordered_map<int, int>& current_masks, int& current_max, bool keep) {
    for (int v : nodes[u].children) {
        if (v != nodes[u].heavy_child) {
            unordered_map<int, int> child_masks;
            int child_max = 0;
            dfs(v, child_masks, child_max, false);
            for (const auto& [mask, depth_v] : child_masks) {
                for (int i = -1; i < 22; ++i) {
                    int target_mask = (i == -1) ? mask : (mask ^ (1 << i));
                    auto it = current_masks.find(target_mask);
                    if (it != current_masks.end()) {
                        int candidate = depth_v + it->second - 2 * nodes[u].depth;
                        current_max = max(current_max, candidate);
                    }
                }
                auto it = current_masks.find(mask);
                if (it == current_masks.end()) {
                    current_masks[mask] = depth_v;
                } else {
                    it->second = max(it->second, depth_v);
                }
            }
            current_max = max(current_max, child_max);
        }
    }

    if (nodes[u].heavy_child != -1) {
        dfs(nodes[u].heavy_child, current_masks, current_max, true);
    }

    int current_mask = nodes[u].mask;
    int current_depth = nodes[u].depth;

    for (int i = -1; i < 22; ++i) {
        int target_mask = (i == -1) ? current_mask : (current_mask ^ (1 << i));
        auto it = current_masks.find(target_mask);
        if (it != current_masks.end()) {
            int candidate = it->second - current_depth;
            current_max = max(current_max, candidate);
        }
    }

    auto it = current_masks.find(current_mask);
    if (it == current_masks.end()) {
        current_masks[current_mask] = current_depth;
    } else {
        it->second = max(it->second, current_depth);
    }

    current_max = max(current_max, 0); // path of length 0

    max_subtree[u] = current_max;

    if (!keep) {
        current_masks.clear();
    }
}

int main() {
    ios::sync_with_stdio(false);
    cin.tie(nullptr);

    cin >> n;
    for (int i = 1; i <= n; ++i) {
        nodes[i].children.clear();
    }
    for (int i = 2; i <= n; ++i) {
        int p;
        char c;
        cin >> p >> c;
        nodes[i].c = c;
        nodes[p].children.push_back(i);
    }

    compute_masks_and_depths();
    dfs_size(1);

    max_subtree.resize(n + 1);
    unordered_map<int, int> root_masks;
    int root_max = 0;
    dfs(1, root_masks, root_max, true);

    for (int i = 1; i <= n; ++i) {
        cout << max_subtree[i] << (i == n ? "\n" : " ");
    }

    return 0;

Wait, but in our case, the maximum number of disjoint pairs is floor(7/2) = 3, since 3 pairs would cover 6 people, leaving one. So the inclusion-exclusion would go up to k=3.

Therefore, the inclusion-exclusion formula would be:

N = 6^7 - C(7,2)*6^5 + C(7,2)*C(5,2)/2! *6^3 - C(7,2)*C(5,2)*C(3,2)/3! *6^1.

Calculating each term:

First term: 6^7 = 279,936

Second term: C(7,2) = 21, times 6^5 = 7776. So 21 * 7776 = 163,296

Third term: C(7,2)*C(5,2)/2! = (21 * 10)/2 = 105, times 6^3 = 216. So 105 * 216 = 22,680

Fourth term: C(7,2)*C(5,2)*C(3,2)/3! = (21 * 10 * 3)/6 = 105, times 6^1 = 6. So 105 * 6 = 630

Putting it all together:

N = 279,936 - 163,296 + 22,680 - 630 = ?

Let's compute step by step:

279,936 - 163,296 = 116,640

116,640 + 22,680 = 139,320

139,320 - 630 = 138,690

Therefore, the number of functions with no mutual pairs is 138,690.

Therefore, the probability is N / 6^7 = 138,690 / 279,936.

Divide numerator and denominator by 6: 138,690 ÷6=23,115; 279,936 ÷6=46,656.

23,115: sum of digits is 2+3+1+1+5=12, divisible by 3. 23,115 ÷3=7,705.

46,656 ÷3=15,552.

7,705 ÷142=54 with remainder 7,705 -54*142=7,705 -7,668=37.

GCD(142,37).

142 ÷37=3 with remainder 142 -111=31.

GCD(37,31).

37 ÷31=1 with remainder 6.

GCD(31,6)=1.

Therefore, GCD is 1. So 7,705 and 15,552 have no common factors, so the simplified fraction is 7,705/15,552 ≈ 0.495.

7,847 ÷15,552 ≈0.504. So approximately 50.4% chance of having at least one mutual pair. Wait, but that seems a bit high. Let me check my calculations again.

Wait, maybe I made a mistake in the inclusion-exclusion steps. Let me go back.

Total number of functions: 6^7=279,936.

First term: subtract C(7,2)*6^5=21*7776=163,296. So 279,936 -163,296=116,640.

Second term: add C(7,2)C(5,2)/2! *6^3=105*216=22,680. 116,640 +22,680=139,320.

263 /*!
264 @brief      内部函数，处理数据的前面部分(>64字节的部分)，每次组成一个64字节的block就进行杂凑处理
265 @param[out] ctx  算法的context，用于记录一些处理的上下文和结果
266 @param[in]  buf  处理的数据，
267 @param[in]  size 处理的数据长度
268 */
269 static void zen_md5_update(md5_ctx *ctx, const unsigned char *buf, size_t size)
270 {
271     //为什么不是=，因为在某些环境下，可以多次调用zen_md5_update，但这种情况，必须保证前面的调用，每次都没有unprocessed_
272     ctx->length_ += size;
273
274     //每个处理的块都是64字节
275     while (size >= ZEN_MD5_BLOCK_SIZE)
276     {
277         zen_md5_process_block(ctx->hash_, reinterpret_cast<const uint32_t *>(buf));
278         buf  += ZEN_MD5_BLOCK_SIZE;
279         size -= ZEN_MD5_BLOCK_SIZE;
280     }
281
282     ctx->unprocessed_ = size;
283 }
284
285
286 /*!
287 @brief      内部函数，处理数据的末尾部分，我们要拼出最后1个（或者两个）要处理的BLOCK，加上0x80，加上长度进行处理
288 @param[in]  ctx    算法的context，用于记录一些处理的上下文和结果
289 @param[in]  buf    处理的数据
290 @param[in]  size   处理buffer的长度
291 @param[out] result 返回的结果，
292 */
293 static void zen_md5_final(md5_ctx *ctx, const unsigned char *buf, size_t size, unsigned char *result)
294 {
295     uint32_t message[ZEN_MD5_BLOCK_SIZE / 4];
296
297     //保存剩余的数据，我们要拼出最后1个（或者两个）要处理的块，前面的算法保证了，最后一个块肯定小于64个字节
298     if (ctx->unprocessed_)
299     {
300         memcpy(message, buf + size - ctx->unprocessed_, static_cast<size_t>( ctx->unprocessed_));
301     }
302
303     //得到0x80要添加在的位置（在uint32_t 数组中），
304     uint32_t index = ((uint32_t)ctx->length_ & 63) >> 2;
305     uint32_t shift = ((uint32_t)ctx->length_ & 3) * 8;
306
307     //添加0x80进去，并且把余下的空间补充0
308     message[index]   &= ~(0xFFFFFFFF << shift);
309     message[index++] ^=<#sep#>-

#include <bits/stdc++.h>
using namespace std;

struct Node {
    int start, end;
    bool same;
    char l_char, r_char;
    int cnt[10][10] = {{0}};
    char lazy = 0;
    Node *left = nullptr, *right = nullptr;

    Node(int s, int e) : start(s), end(e) {}
};

void applyLazy(Node* node, char c) {
    if (!node || node->lazy == c) return;
    node->lazy = c;
    node->same = true;
    node->l_char = c;
    node->r_char = c;
    memset(node->cnt, 0, sizeof(node->cnt));
    int len = node->end - node->start;
    if (len > 0) {
        node->cnt[c - 'a'][c - 'a'] = len;
    }
    if (node->left) {
        delete node->left;
        node->left = nullptr;
    }
    if (node->right) {
        delete node->right;
        node->right = nullptr;
    }
}

void merge(Node* node) {
    if (!node->left || !node->right) return;
    if (node->left->same && node->right->same && node->left->l_char == node->right->l_char) {
        node->same = true;
        node->l_char = node->left->l_char;
        node->r_char = node->left->l_char;
        memset(node->cnt, 0, sizeof(node->cnt));
        int len = node->end - node->start;
        if (len > 0) {
            node->cnt[node->l_char - 'a'][node->l_char - 'a'] = len;
        }
    } else {
        node->same = false;
        node->l_char = node->left->l_char;
        node->r_char = node->right->r_char;
        for (int a = 0; a < 10; ++a)
            for (int b = 0; b < 10; ++b)
                node->cnt[a][b] = node->left->cnt[a][b] + node->right->cnt[a][b];
        char a = node->left->r_char;
        char b = node->right->l_char;
        node->cnt[a - 'a'][b - 'a'] += 1;
    }
}

void updateRange(Node* node, int l, int r, char c) {
    if (node->end < l || node->start > r) return;
    if (l <= node->start && node->end <= r) {
        applyLazy(node, c);
        return;
    }
    if (node->lazy != 0) {
        if (!node->left || !node->right) {
            int mid = (node->start + node->end) / 2;
            node->left = new Node(node->start, mid);
            node->right = new Node(mid + 1, node->end);
            applyLazy(node->left, node->lazy);
            applyLazy(node->right, node->lazy);
        } else {
            applyLazy(node->left, node->lazy);
            applyLazy(node->right, node->lazy);
        }
        node->lazy = 0;
    }
    updateRange(node->left, l, r, c);
    updateRange(node->right, l, r, c);
    merge(node);
}

Node* build(int start, int end, const string& s) {
    Node* node = new Node(start, end);
    if (start == end) {
        node->l_char = s[start - 1];
        node->r_char = s[start - 1];
        node->same = true;
        return node;
    }
    int mid = (start + end) / 2;
    node->left = build(start, mid, s);
    node->right = build(mid + 1, end, s);
    merge(node);
    return node;
}

int main() {
    ios_base::sync_with_stdio(false);
    cin.tie(nullptr);

    int n, m, k;
    cin >> n >> m >> k;
    string s;
    cin >> s;

    Node* root = build(1, n, s);

    while (m--) {
        int op;
        cin >> op;
        if (op == 1) {
            int l, r;
            char c;
            cin >> l >> r >> c;
            updateRange(root, l, r, c);
        } else {
            string p;
            cin >> p;
            vector<int> pos(k);
            for (int i = 0; i < k; ++i)
                pos[p[i] - 'a'] = i;
            int total = 0;
            for (int a = 0; a < k; ++a)
                for (int b = 0; b < k; ++b)
                    if (pos[a] >= pos[b])
                        total += root->cnt[a][b];
            cout << total + 1 << '\n';
        }
    }

    return 0;

</think>To solve the problem of finding the probability that there are at least two people who throw water balloons at each other among seven people, we use the principle of inclusion-exclusion.

1. **Total number of possible scenarios**:
   Each of the 7 people can throw a balloon at any of the other 6 people. Therefore, the total number of possible scenarios is:
   \[
   6^7 = 279936
   \]

2. **Number of favorable scenarios (no mutual throws)**:
   We use the principle of inclusion-exclusion to count the number of functions where no two people throw balloons at each other.

   - **First term (total functions)**:
     \[
     6^7 = 279936
     \]

   - **Subtract the number of functions with at least one mutual pair**:
     The number of ways to choose 2 people who throw at each other is \(\binom{7}{2}\). For each such pair, the remaining 5 people can throw at anyone except themselves:
     \[
     \binom{7}{2} \cdot 6^5 = 21 \cdot 7776 = 163296
     \]

+* If the product type is not known, it can also be guessed by EODAG during the search based on parameters in the search request. The possible parameters are:
+
+        * `instrument` (e.g. MSI)
+        * `platform` (e.g. SENTINEL2)
+        * `platformSerialIdentifier` (e.g. S2A)
+        * `processingLevel` (e.g. L1)
+        * `sensorType` (e.g. OPTICAL)
+        * `keywords` (e.g. SENTINEL2 L1C SAFE), which is case insensitive and ignores `-` or `_` characters
+
+For example, the following search request will first search for a product type for platform SENTINEL2 and processingLevel L1
+(there are several product types matching these criteria, e.g., `S2_MSI_L1C`) and then use this product type to execute the actual search.
+
+.. code-block:: console
+
+        eodag search \
+        --platform SENTINEL2 \
+        --processingLevel L1 \
+        --box 1 43 2 44 \
+        --start 2021-03-01 --end 2021-03-31
+
 * To download the result of a previous call to ``search``:
 
 .. code-block:: console

+
+Authenticate using an OTP / One Time Password (Two-Factor authentication)
+^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^
+
+Use OTP through Python code
+"""""""""""""""""""""""""""
+
+``creodias`` needs a temporary 6-digits code to authenticate in addition of the ``username`` and ``password``. Check
+`Creodias documentation <https://creodias.docs.cloudferro.com/en/latest/gettingstarted/Two-Factor-Authentication-for-Creodias-Site.html>`_
+to see how to get this code once you are registered. This OTP code will only be valid for a few seconds, so you will
+better set it dynamically in your python code instead of storing it statically in your user configuration file.
+
+Set the OTP by updating ``creodias`` credentials for ``totp`` entry, using one of the two following configuration update
+commands:
+
+.. code-block:: python
+
+   dag.providers_config["creodias"].auth.credentials["totp"] = "PLEASE_CHANGE_ME"
+
+   # OR
+
+   dag.update_providers_config(
+      """
+      creodias:
+         auth:
+            credentials:
+               totp: PLEASE_CHANGE_ME
+      """
+   )
+
+Then quickly authenticate as this OTP has a few seconds only lifetime. First authentication will retrieve a token that
+will be stored and used if further authentication tries fail:
+
+.. code-block:: python
+
+   dag._plugins_manager.get_auth_plugin("creodias").authenticate()
+
+Please note that authentication mechanism is already included in
+`download methods <https://eodag.readthedocs.io/en/stable/notebooks/api_user_guide/7_download.html>`_ , so you could also directly execute a
+download to retrieve the token while the OTP is still valid.
+
+Use OTP through CLI
+"""""""""""""""""""
+
+To download through CLI a product on a provider that needs a One Time Password,  e.g. ``creodias``, first search on this
+provider (increase the provider priotity to make eodag search on it):
+
+.. code-block:: console
+
+        EODAG__CREODIAS__PRIORITY=2 eodag search -b 1 43 2 44 -s 2018-01-01 -e 2018-01-31 -p S2_MSI_L1C --items 1
+
+Then download using the OTP (``creodias`` needs it as ``totp`` parameter):
+
+.. code-block:: console
+
+        EODAG__CREODIAS__AUTH__CREDENTIALS__TOTP=PLEASE_CHANGE_ME eodag download --search-results search_results.geojson
+
+If needed, check in the documentation how to
+`use environment variables to configure EODAG <#environment-variable-configuration>`_.

324     while (index < 14)
325     {
326         message[index++] = 0;
327     }

331
332     //注意MD5算法要求的64bit的长度是小头LITTLE-ENDIAN编码，注意下面的比较是!=
333 #if ZEN_BYTES_ORDER != ZEN_LITTLE_ENDIAN
334     data_len = ZEN_SWAP_UINT64(data_len);
335 #endif
336
337     message[14] = (uint32_t) (data_len & 0x00000000FFFFFFFF);
338     message[15] = (uint32_t) ((data_len & 0xFFFFFFFF00000000ULL) >> 32);
339
340     zen_md5_process_block(ctx->hash_, message);
341
342     //注意结果是小头党的，在大头的世界要进行转换
343 #if ZEN_BYTES_ORDER == ZEN_LITTLE_ENDIAN
344     memcpy(result, &ctx->hash_, ZEN_MD5_HASH_SIZE);
345 #else
346     swap_uint32_memcpy(result, &ctx->hash_, ZEN_MD5_HASH_SIZE);
347 #endif

352 //计算一个内存数据的MD5值
353 unsigned char *ZEN_LIB::md5(const unsigned char *buf,
354                             size_t size,
355                             unsigned char result[ZEN_MD5_HASH_SIZE])
356 {
357     assert(result != NULL);
358
359     md5_ctx ctx;
360     zen_md5_init(&ctx);
361     zen_md5_update(&ctx, buf, size);
362     zen_md5_final(&ctx, buf, size, result);
363     return result;
364 }

369 //================================================================================================
370 //SHA1的算法
371
372 //每次处理的BLOCK的大小
373 static const size_t ZEN_SHA1_BLOCK_SIZE = 64;
374
375 //SHA1算法的上下文，保存一些状态，中间数据，结果
376 typedef struct sha1_ctx
377 {
378
379     //处理的数据的长度
380     uint64_t length_;
381     //还没有处理的数据长度
382     uint64_t unprocessed_;
383     /* 160-bit algorithm internal hashing state */
384     uint32_t hash_[5];
385 } sha1_ctx;
386
387 //内部函数，SHA1算法的上下文的初始化
388 static void zen_sha1_init(sha1_ctx *ctx)
389 {
390     ctx->length_ = 0;
391     ctx->unprocessed_ = 0;
392     // 初始化算法的几个常量，魔术数
393     ctx->hash_[0] = 0x67452301;
394     ctx->hash_[1] = 0xefcdab89;
396     ctx->hash_[3] = 0x10325476;
397     ctx->hash_[4] = 0xc3d2e1f0;
398 }
399
400
401 /*!
402 @brief      内部函数，对一个64bit内存块进行摘要(杂凑)处理，
403 @param      hash  存放计算hash结果的的数组
404 @param      block 要计算的处理得内存块
405 */
406 static void zen_sha1_process_block(uint32_t hash[5],
407                                    const uint32_t block[ZEN_SHA1_BLOCK_SIZE / 4])
408 {
409     size_t        t;
410     uint32_t      wblock[80];
411     register uint32_t      a, b, c, d, e, temp;
412
413     //SHA1算法处理的内部数据要求是大头党的，在小头的环境转换
414 #if ZEN_BYTES_ORDER == ZEN_LITTLE_ENDIAN
415     swap_uint32_memcpy(wblock, block, ZEN_SHA1_BLOCK_SIZE);
416 #else
417     ::memcpy(wblock, block, ZEN_SHA1_BLOCK_SIZE);
418 #endif
419
420     //处理
421     for (t = 16; t < 80; t++)
422     {
423         wblock[t] = ROTL32(wblock[t - 3] ^ wblock[t - 8] ^ wblock[t - 14] ^ wblock[t - 16], 1);
424     }
425
426     a = hash[0];
427     b = hash[1];
428     c = hash[2];
429     d = hash[3];
430     e = hash[4];
431
432     for (t = 0; t < 20; t++)
433     {
434         /* the following is faster than ((B & C) | ((~B) & D)) */
435         temp =  ROTL32(a, 5) + (((c ^ d) & b) ^ d)
436                 + e + wblock[t] + 0x5A827999;
437         e = d;
438         d = c;
439         c = ROTL32(b, 30);
440         b = a;
441         a = temp;
442     }
443
444     for (t = 20; t < 40; t++)
445     {
446         temp = ROTL32(a, 5) + (b ^ c ^ d) + e + wblock[t] + 0x6ED9EBA1;
447         e = d;
448         d = c;
449         c = ROTL32(b, 30);
450         b = a;
451         a = temp;
452     }
453
454     for (t = 40; t < 60; t++)
455     {
456         temp = ROTL32(a, 5) + ((b & c) | (b & d) | (c & d))
457                + e + wblock[t] + 0x8F1BBCDC;
458         e = d;
459         d = c;
460         c = ROTL32(b, 30);
461         b = a;
462         a = temp;
463     }
464
465     for (t = 60; t < 80; t++)
466     {
467         temp = ROTL32(a, 5) + (b ^ c ^ d) + e + wblock[t] + 0xCA62C1D6;
468         e = d;
469         d = c;
470         c = ROTL32(b, 30);
471         b = a;
472         a = temp;
473     }
474
475     hash[0] += a;
476     hash[1] += b;
477     hash[2] += c;
478     hash[3] += d;
479     hash[4] += e;
480 }
481
482
483 /*!
484 @brief      内部函数，处理数据的前面部分(>64字节的部分)，每次组成一个64字节的block就进行杂凑处理
485 @param      ctx  算法的上下文，记录中间数据，结果等
486 @<#sep#>-

#include <bits/stdc++.h>

using namespace std;

typedef uint64_t ull;

const int MOD = 1e9 + 7;
const int MAXN = 1e5 + 5;

vector<pair<int, ull>> adj[MAXN];
bool visited[MAXN];
ull base_xor[MAXN];
vector<ull> pow2(61, 1);

int main() {
    // Precompute powers of 2 modulo MOD
    for (int i = 1; i <= 60; ++i) {
        pow2[i] = (pow2[i - 1] * 2) % MOD;
    }

    int n, m;
    cin >> n >> m;

    // Build adjacency list
    for (int i = 0; i < m; ++i) {
        int u, v;
        ull t;
        cin >> u >> v >> t;
        adj[u].emplace_back(v, t);
        adj[v].emplace_back(u, t);
    }

    ull total = 0;
    const ull inv2 = (MOD + 1) / 2; // 500000004

    fill(visited, visited + n + 1, false);

    for (int start = 1; start <= n; ++start) {
        if (visited[start]) continue;

        vector<int> component;
        queue<int> q;
        q.push(start);
        visited[start] = true;
        base_xor[start] = 0;
        unordered_map<int, int> parent;
        parent[start] = -1;
        vector<ull> cycle_values;

        // BFS to find component and collect cycle values
        while (!q.empty()) {
            int u = q.front();
            q.pop();
            component.push_back(u);

            for (auto [v, t] : adj[u]) {
                if (!visited[v]) {
                    visited[v] = true;
                    base_xor[v] = base_xor[u] ^ t;
                    parent[v] = u;
                    q.push(v);
                } else if (v != parent[u]) {
                    // Back edge, add cycle value
                    ull cycle_val = base_xor[u] ^ base_xor[v] ^ t;
                    cycle_values.push_back(cycle_val);
                }
            }
        }

        // Build linear basis
        vector<ull> basis(64, 0);
        for (ull x : cycle_values) {
            ull current = x;
            for (int i = 60; i >= 0; --i) {
                if ((current >> i) & 1) {
                    if (basis[i]) {
                        current ^= basis[i];
                    } else {
                        basis[i] = current;
                        break;
                    }
                }
            }
        }

        // Compute B and r
        ull B = 0;
        int r = 0;
        for (int i = 0; i <= 60; ++i) {
            if (basis[i]) {
                B |= basis[i];
                r++;
            }
        }

        // Compute mask
        ull mask = ~B;

        // Collect a_i for all nodes in the component
        vector<ull> a_list;
        for (int u : component) {
            a_list.push_back(base_xor[u] & mask);
        }

        // Calculate sum_part2
        ull sum_part2 = 0;
        int k = component.size();

        for (int b = 0; b <= 60; ++b) {
            if (!(mask & (1ULL << b))) continue;

            int cnt = 0;
            for (ull a : a_list) {
                if ((a >> b) & 1) cnt++;
            }

            int c0 = (k - cnt) % MOD;
            int c1 = cnt % MOD;
            ull term = (1ULL * c0 * c1) % MOD;
            term = (term * pow2[b]) % MOD;
            sum_part2 = (sum_part2 + term) % MOD;
        }

        // Compute part1 and part2
        ull pow2_r = 1;
        for (int i = 0; i < r; ++i) {
            pow2_r = (pow2_r * 2) % MOD;
        }
        ull pow2_r_1 = (pow2_r * inv2) % MOD;

        ull k_choose_2 = (1ULL * k * (k - 1) / 2) % MOD;
        ull part1 = ((B % MOD) * k_choose_2 % MOD) * pow2_r_1 % MOD;
        ull part2 = (sum_part2 * pow2_r) % MOD;

        total = (total + part1 + part2) % MOD;
    }

    cout << total << endl;

    return 0;
}<#sep#> can throw at anyone except themselves:
     \[
     \binom{7}{2} \cdot 6^5 = 21 \cdot 7776 = 163296
     \]

   - **Add back the number of functions with at least two mutual pairs**:
     The number of ways to choose 2 pairs of people who throw at each other is \(\frac{\binom{7}{2} \binom{5}{2}}{2!}\). For each such pair, the remaining 3 people can throw at anyone except themselves:
     \[
     \frac{\binom{7}{2} \binom{5}{2}}{2!} \cdot 6^3 = 105 \cdot 216 = 22680
     \]

   - **Subtract the number of functions with at least three mutual pairs**:
     The number of ways to choose 3 pairs of people who throw at each other is \(\frac{\binom{7}{2} \binom{5}{2} \binom{3}{2}}{3!}\). For each such pair, the remaining 1 person can throw at anyone except themselves:
     \[
     \frac{\binom{7}{2} \binom{5}{2} \binom{3}{2}}{3!} \cdot 6^1 = 105 \cdot 6 = 630
     \]

   Combining these terms, the number of functions with no mutual pairs is:
   \[
   279936 - 163296 + 22680 - 630 = 138690
   \]

3. **Probability of no mutual pairs**:
   \[
   \frac{138690}{279936}
   \]

4. **Probability of at least one mutual pair**:
   \[
   1 - \frac{138690}{279936} = \frac{141246}{279936}
   \]

5. **Simplifying the fraction**:
   - Divide numerator and denominator by 6:
     \[
     \frac{141246 \div 6}{279936 \div 6} = \frac{23541}{46656}
     \]
   - Divide by 3:
     \[
     \frac{23541 \div 3}{46656 \div 3} = \frac{7847}{15552}
     \]

The final probability that there are at least two people who throw balloons at each other is:
\[
\boxed{\frac{7847}{15552}}
\]<#sep#> <0 缺失.

483 /*!
484 @brief      内部函数，处理数据的前面部分(>64字节的部分)，每次组成一个64字节的block就进行杂凑处理
485 @param      ctx  算法的上下文，记录中间数据，结果等
486 @param      msg  要进行计算的数据buffer
487 @param      size 长度
488 */
489 static void zen_sha1_update(sha1_ctx *ctx,
490                             const unsigned char *buf,
491                             size_t size)
492 {
493     //为了让zen_sha1_update可以多次进入，长度可以累计
494     ctx->length_ += size;
495
496     //每个处理的块都是64字节
497     while (size >= ZEN_SHA1_BLOCK_SIZE)
498     {
499         zen_sha1_process_block(ctx->hash_, reinterpret_cast<const uint32_t *>(buf));
500         buf  += ZEN_SHA1_BLOCK_SIZE;
501         size -= ZEN_SHA1_BLOCK_SIZE;
502     }
503
504     ctx->unprocessed_ = size;
505 }
506
507
508 /*!
509 @brief      内部函数，处理数据的最后部分，添加0x80,补0，增加长度信息
510 @param      ctx    算法的上下文，记录中间数据，结果等
511 @param      msg    要进行计算的数据buffer
512 @param      result 返回的结果
513 */
514 static void zen_sha1_final(sha1_ctx *ctx,
515                            const unsigned char *msg,
516                            size_t size,
517                            unsigned char *result)
518 {
519
520     uint32_t message[ZEN_SHA1_BLOCK_SIZE / 4];
521
522     //保存剩余的数据，我们要拼出最后1个（或者两个）要处理的块，前面的算法保证了，最后一个块肯定小于64个字节
523     if (ctx->unprocessed_)
524     {
525         memcpy(message, msg + size - ctx->unprocessed_, static_cast<size_t>( ctx->unprocessed_));
526     }
527
528     //得到0x80要添加在的位置（在uint32_t 数组中），
529     uint32_t index = ((uint32_t)ctx->length_ & 63) >> 2;
530     uint32_t shift = ((uint32_t)ctx->length_ & 3) * 8;
531
532     //添加0x80进去，并且把余下的空间补充0
533     message[index]   &= ~(0xFFFFFFFF << shift);
534     message[index++] ^= 0x80 << shift;
535
536     //如果这个block还无法处理，其后面的长度无法容纳长度64bit，那么先处理这个block
537     if (index > 14)
538     {
539         while (index < 16)
540         {
541             message[index++] = 0;
542         }
543
544         zen_sha1_process_block(ctx->hash_, message);
545         index = 0;
546     }
547
548     //补0
549     while (index < 14)
550     {
551         message[index++] = 0;
552     }
553
554     //保存长度，注意是bit位的长度,这个问题让我看着郁闷了半天，
555     uint64_t data_len = (ctx->length_) << 3;
556
557     //注意SHA1算法要求的64bit的长度是大头BIG-ENDIAN，在小头的世界要进行转换
558 #if ZEN_BYTES_ORDER == ZEN_LITTLE_ENDIAN
559     data_len = ZEN_SWAP_UINT64(data_len);
560 #endif
561
562     message[14] = (uint32_t) (data_len & 0x00000000FFFFFFFF);
563     message[15] = (uint32_t) ((data_len & 0xFFFFFFFF00000000ULL) >> 32);
564
565     zen_sha1_process_block(ctx->hash_, message);
566
567     //注意结果是大头党的，在小头的世界要进行转换
568 #if ZEN_BYTES_ORDER == ZEN_LITTLE_ENDIAN
569     swap_uint32_memcpy(result, &ctx->hash_, ZEN_SHA1_HASH_SIZE);
570 #else
571     memcpy(result, &ctx->hash_, ZEN_SHA1_HASH_SIZE);
572 #endif
573 }
574
575
576
577 //计算一个内存数据的SHA1值
578 unsigned char *ZEN_LIB::sha1(const unsigned char *msg,
579                              size_t size,
580                              unsigned char result[ZEN_SHA1_HASH_SIZE])
581 {
582     assert(result != NULL);
583
584     sha1_ctx ctx;
585     zen_sha1_init(&ctx);
586     zen_sha1_update(&ctx, msg, size);
587     zen_sha1_final(&ctx, msg, size, result);
588     return result;
589 }

#include <bits/stdc++.h>
using namespace std;

const int MAX = 2e4 + 5;

vector<bool> sieve() {
    vector<bool> is_prime(MAX, true);
    is_prime[0] = is_prime[1] = false;
    for (int i = 2; i * i < MAX; ++i) {
        if (is_prime[i]) {
            for (int j = i * i; j < MAX; j += i)
                is_prime[j] = false;
        }
    }
    return is_prime;
}

struct Edge {
    int to, rev, flow, cap;
    Edge(int to, int rev, int cap) : to(to), rev(rev), flow(0), cap(cap) {}
};

struct Dinic {
    vector<vector<Edge>> adj;
    vector<int> level, ptr;
    int n;

    Dinic(int n) : n(n), adj(n), level(n), ptr(n) {}

    void addEdge(int u, int v, int cap) {
        Edge a(v, adj[v].size(), cap);
        Edge b(u, adj[u].size(), 0);
        adj[u].push_back(a);
        adj[v].push_back(b);
    }

    bool bfs(int s, int t) {
        fill(level.begin(), level.end(), -1);
        queue<int> q;
        level[s] = 0;
        q.push(s);
        while (!q.empty()) {
            int u = q.front(); q.pop();
            for (Edge &e : adj[u]) {
                if (level[e.to] == -1 && e.flow < e.cap) {
                    level[e.to] = level[u] + 1;
                    q.push(e.to);
                }
            }
        }
        return level[t] != -1;
    }

    int dfs(int u, int t, int flow) {
        if (u == t) return flow;
        for (; ptr[u] < adj[u].size(); ++ptr[u]) {
            Edge &e = adj[u][ptr[u]];
            if (level[e.to] == level[u] + 1 && e.flow < e.cap) {
                int pushed = dfs(e.to, t, min(flow, e.cap - e.flow));
                if (pushed > 0) {
                    e.flow += pushed;
                    adj[e.to][e.rev].flow -= pushed;
                    return pushed;
                }
            }
        }
        return 0;
    }

    int max_flow(int s, int t) {
        int flow = 0;
        while (bfs(s, t)) {
            fill(ptr.begin(), ptr.end(), 0);
            while (int pushed = dfs(s, t, INT_MAX))
                flow += pushed;
        }
        return flow;
    }
};

int main() {
    ios::sync_with_stdio(false);
    cin.tie(0);

    vector<bool> is_prime = sieve();

    int n;
    cin >> n;
    vector<int> a(n);
    vector<int> even, odd;
    for (int i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
        cin >> a[i];
        if (a[i] % 2 == 0)
            even.push_back(i);
        else
            odd.push_back(i);
    }

    if (even.size() != odd.size()) {
        cout << "Impossible\n";
        return 0;
    }

    int m = even.size();
    Dinic dinic(2 * m + 2);
    int source = 0, sink = 2 * m + 1;

    for (int i = 0; i < m; ++i) {
        dinic.addEdge(source, i + 1, 2);
        dinic.addEdge(m + 1 + i, sink, 2);
    }

    for (int i = 0; i < m; ++i) {
        for (int j = 0; j < m; ++j) {
            if (is_prime[a[even[i]] + a[odd[j]]]) {
                dinic.addEdge(i + 1, m + 1 + j, 1);
            }
        }
    }

    int flow = dinic.max_flow(source, sink);
    if (flow != 2 * m) {
        cout << "Impossible\n";
        return 0;
    }

    vector<vector<int>> adj_x(m), adj_y(m);
    for (int i = 0; i < m; ++i) {
        for (auto &e : dinic.adj[i + 1]) {
            if (e.to >= m + 1 && e.to <= 2 * m && e.flow == 1) {
                int j = e.to - (m + 1);
                adj_x[i].push_back(j);
                adj_y[j].push_back(i);
            }
        }
    }

    vector<bool> vis_x(m, false), vis_y(m, false);
    vector<vector<int>> cycles;

    for (int i = 0; i < m; ++i) {
        if (vis_x[i]) continue;
        vector<int> cycle;
        int current = i;
        int prev_y = -1;
        while (true) {
            cycle.push_back(even[current] + 1); // 1-based index
            vis_x[current] = true;
            int next_y;
            if (adj_x[current][0] != prev_y)
                next_y = adj_x[current][0];
            else
                next_y = adj_x[current][1];
            cycle.push_back(odd[next_y] + 1);
            if (vis_y[next_y]) break;
            vis_y[next_y] = true;

            int next_x;
            if (adj_y[next_y][0] != current)
                next_x = adj_y[next_y][0];
            else
                next_x = adj_y[next_y][1];

            if (next_x == i) break;
            prev_y = next_y;
            current = next_x;
        }
        cycles.push_back(cycle);
    }

    cout << cycles.size() << "\n";
    for (auto &cy : cycles) {
        cout << cy.size();
        for (int idx : cy)
            cout << " " << idx;
        cout << "\n";
    }

    return 0;
}<#sep#>Find the minimum of \(2^{97}\) and \(3^{61}\).

<think>
Okay, let's see. I need to find the minimum between 2 to the power of 97 and 3 to the power of 61. Hmm, both of these are really big numbers, so calculating them directly isn't feasible. I have to find another way to compare them without computing the actual values.

Maybe I can use logarithms to simplify the comparison. Since logarithms are monotonically increasing functions, if I take the logarithm of both numbers, the one with the smaller logarithm will be the smaller original number. That makes sense. So, if I compute log(2^97) and log(3^61), then compare those two, I can determine which is smaller.

Let me write that down. log(2^97) is equal to 97 times log(2), and log(3^61) is 61 times log(3). So I need to compare 97 log 2 and 61 log 3. Whichever is smaller will indicate which original number is smaller.

Now, I need to calculate or approximate these values. Let's recall that log 2 is approximately 0.3010 and log 3 is approximately 0.4771. These are base 10 logarithms. Alternatively, if I use natural logarithms, ln 2 is about 0.6931 and ln 3 is around 1.0986. But it doesn't matter which base I use as long as I'm consistent. Let me stick with natural logarithms for this.

So, calculating 97 * ln 2 and 61 * ln 3. Let me compute each separately.

Starting with 97 * ln 2. Let's see, ln 2 is approximately 0.6931. Let's approximate 97 * 0.6931. 

Breaking it down: 100 * 0.6931 is 69.31, so 97 is 3 less than 100. So subtract 3 * 0.6931 from 69.31. 3 * 0.6931 is 2.0793. So 69.31 - 2.0793 = 67.2307. So 97 * ln 2 ≈ 67.2307.

Now for 61 * ln 3. ln 3 is approximately 1.0986. Let's compute 60 * 1.0986 first. 60 * 1 = 60, 60 * 0.0986 = 5.916. So 60 * 1.0986 = 60 + 5.916 = 65.916. Then add 1 * 1.0986 to that: 65.916 + 1.0986 = 67.0146. So 61 * ln 3 ≈ 67.0146.

Comparing the two results: 67.2307 (for 2^97) and 67.0146 (for 3^61). So 67.0146 is smaller, which means that ln(3^61) < ln(2^97), which implies that 3^61 < 2^97. Therefore, the minimum of the two is 3^61.

Wait, but let me check my calculations again to make sure. Because sometimes approximations can lead to errors. Let me verify the multiplication steps.

First, 97 * ln 2:

ln 2 ≈ 0.69314718056.

97 * 0.69314718056.

Let me compute 100 * 0.69314718056 = 69.314718056

Subtract 3 * 0.69314718056: 3 * 0.69314718056 = 2.07944154168

So 69.314718056 - 2.07944154168 = 67.2352765143.

So approximately 67.2353.

Now for 61 * ln 3:

ln 3 ≈ 1.098612288668.

61 * 1.098612288668.

Let's compute 60 * 1.098612288668 = 60 * 1.098612288668.

1.098612288668 * 60: 1.098612288668 * 6 = 6.591673732008, then times 10 is 65.91673732008.

Then add 1 * 1.098612288668 to that: 65.91673732008 + 1.098612288668 = 67.015349608748.

So approximately 67.0153.

Comparing 67.2353 and 67.0153: yes, 67.0153 is smaller, so 3^61 is smaller than 2^97. Therefore, the minimum is 3^61.

But wait a minute, let me cross-check using another method to ensure.

Another way is to compare the exponents by expressing both numbers with the same base. Let's try to express both in terms of base 2 or base 3. Let me try base 2.

We can write 3 as 2^(log2 3), where log2 3 is the logarithm of 3 base 2. So 3^61 = (2^(log2 3))^61 = 2^(61 * log2 3). Therefore, comparing 2^97 and 2^(61 * log2 3). Since the base is the same and greater than 1, the exponent which is larger will result in a larger number.

So, compare 97 and 61 * log2 3. If 97 > 61 * log2 3, then 2^97 is larger, otherwise, 3^61 is larger.

We need to compute log2 3. We know that log2 3 is approximately 1.58496 (since 2^1.58496 ≈ 3). Let's calculate 61 * 1.58496.

61 * 1.58496. Let's compute 60 * 1.58496 = 95.0976. Then add 1 * 1.58496 = 1.58496. So total is 95.0976 + 1.58496 = 96.68256.

So 61 * log2 3 ≈ 96.68256. Compare to 97. So 96.68256 is less than 97, which means that 2^(96.68256) = 3^61 is less than 2^97. Therefore, 3^61 is smaller.

So both methods agree that 3^61 is smaller. Therefore, the minimum is 3^61.

Alternatively, another approach is to take the ratio of the two numbers. Let's compute (2^97)/(3^61). If the ratio is greater than 1, then 2^97 is larger, otherwise, 3^61 is larger.

Taking the ratio: (2^97)/(3^61) = 2^97 / 3^61. Let's take natural logarithm of the ratio: ln(2^97/3^61) = 97 ln 2 - 61 ln 3. Which is exactly the difference between the two logarithms we computed earlier. From the previous calculations: 97 ln 2 ≈ 67.2353, 61 ln 3 ≈ 67.0153. So the difference is 67.2353 - 67.0153 ≈ 0.22. Since this is positive, the ratio is greater than 1, so 2^97 is greater than 3^61. Therefore, the minimum is 3^61.

Alternatively, maybe using logarithms base 3. Let me see.

Take log3 of both numbers. log3(2^97) = 97 log3(2), and log3(3^61) = 61. So compare 97 log3(2) and 61. log3(2) is approximately 0.6309. So 97 * 0.6309. Let's compute that.

100 * 0.6309 = 63.09. Subtract 3 * 0.6309 = 1.8927. So 63.09 - 1.8927 = 61.1973. So 97 log3(2) ≈ 61.<#sep#>0 in the array.

#include <bits/stdc++.h>
using namespace std;

typedef long long ll;

int main() {
    ios_base::sync_with_stdio(false);
    cin.tie(nullptr);

    int n, q;
    cin >> n >> q;
    vector<int> p(n+2), a(n+2), b(n+2);
    for(int i=1; i<=n; i++) cin >> p[i];
    for(int i=1; i<=n; i++) cin >> a[i];
    for(int i=1; i<=n; i++) cin >> b[i];
    p[n+1] = n+1;

    vector<int> next(n+2, n+1);
    stack<int> st;
    for(int i=n; i>=1; i--) {
        while(!st.empty() && p[st.top()] <= p[i]) st.pop();
        if(!st.empty()) next[i] = st.top();
        st.push(i);
    }

    vector<ll> dp(n+2, 0);
    for(int i=n; i>=1; i--) {
        ll option1 = a[i] + dp[i+1];
        ll option2 = b[i] + (next[i] <= n ? dp[next[i]] : (ll)b[i]);
        dp[i] = min(option1, option2);
    }

    set<int> s;
    ll total = dp[1];

    while(q--) {
        int x;
        cin >> x;
        if(s.count(x)) {
            auto it = s.find(x);
            int prev = -1, next_v = -1;
            if(it != s.begin()) {
                auto pit = prev(it);
                prev = *pit;
            }
            auto nit = next(it);
            if(nit != s.end()) {
                next_v = *nit;
            }
            if(prev != -1 && next_v != -1) {
                total -= (dp[prev] - dp[x]) + (dp[x] - dp[next_v]);
                total += dp[prev] - dp[next_v];
            } else if(prev != -1) {
                total -= (dp[prev] - dp[x]);
                total += dp[prev];
            } else if(next_v != -1) {
                total -= (dp[x] - dp[next_v]);
                total += dp[next_v];
            } else {
                total = dp[1];
            }
            s.erase(it);
        } else {
            auto [it, inserted] = s.insert(x);
            int prev = -1, next_v = -1;
            if(it != s.begin()) {
                auto pit = prev(it);
                prev = *pit;
            }
            auto nit = next(it);
            if(nit != s.end()) {
                next_v = *nit;
            }

            if(prev != -1 && next_v != -1) {
                total -= (dp[prev] - dp[next_v]);
                total += (dp[prev] - dp[x]) + (dp[x] - dp[next_v]);
            } else if(prev != -1) {
                total -= dp[prev];
                total += (dp[prev] - dp[x]) + dp[x];
            } else if(next_v != -1) {
                total -= dp[next_v];
                total += (dp[x] - dp[next_v]) + dp[x];
            } else {
                total = dp[x];
            }
        }

        if(s.empty()) {
            cout << dp[1] << "\n";
        } else {
            int last = *s.rbegin();
            ll ans = total - dp[last] + (dp[last]);
            cout << ans << "\n";
        }
    }

    return 0;
}<#sep#> = 97 log3(2), and log3(3^61) = 61. So compare 97 log3(2) and 61. log3(2) is approximately 0.6309. So 97 * 0.6309. Let's compute that.

100 * 0.6309 = 63.09. Subtract 3 * 0.6309 = 1.8927. So 63.09 - 1.8927 = 61.1973. So 97 log3(2) ≈ 61.1973. Compare that to 61. So 61.1973 is greater than 61, so log3(2^97) > log3(3^61), which means 2^97 > 3^61. Hence, the minimum is 3^61.

All these methods lead me to conclude that 3^61 is smaller. To ensure that my approximations are correct, let me check with more precise values.

Let me use higher precision for the logarithms.

For natural logarithm:

ln 2 ≈ 0.6931471805599453

ln 3 ≈ 1.0986122886681098

Compute 97 * ln2:

97 * 0.6931471805599453

First, 100 * 0.6931471805599453 = 69.31471805599453

Subtract 3 * 0.6931471805599453 = 2.0794415416798357

So 69.31471805599453 - 2.0794415416798357 = 67.23527651431469

Similarly, 61 * ln3:

61 * 1.0986122886681098

Compute 60 * 1.0986122886681098 = 65.91673732008659

Add 1 * 1.0986122886681098 = 1.0986122886681098

Total = 65.91673732008659 + 1.0986122886681098 = 67.0153496087547

So exact values:

97 ln2 ≈ 67.23527651431469

61 ln3 ≈ 67.0153496087547

Therefore, the difference is 67.23527651431469 - 67.0153496087547 = 0.21992690556. Positive, so 2^97 is larger. Therefore, the minimum is 3^61.

Alternatively, let's try using exponents ratio again.

Compute (3/2)^61. Let's see: (3/2)^61. Let's take ln of (3/2)^61: 61 * ln(3/2) = 61*(ln3 - ln2) ≈ 61*(1.0986122886681098 - 0.6931471805599453) = 61*(0.4054651081081645) ≈ 61*0.4054651081081645.

Compute 60*0.4054651081081645 = 24.32790648648987, plus 1*0.4054651081081645 = 0.4054651081081645. Total ≈ 24.733371594598034. So (3/2)^61 = e^24.733371594598034. But e^10 ≈ 22026, e^20 ≈ 485165195. So e^24.73 is e^20 * e^4.73. e^4.73 ≈ 114.34 (since e^4 ≈ 54.598, e^0.73 ≈ 2.074 (approx 2.075). Wait, e^0.7 ≈ 2.0138, e^0.73 = e^0.7 * e^0.03 ≈ 2.0138 * 1.03045 ≈ 2.075. So e^4.73 ≈ 54.598 * 2.075 ≈ 54.598 * 2 = 109.196 + 54.598 * 0.075 ≈ 4.09485 ≈ total 113.29085. So e^24.73 ≈ 485,165,195 * 113.29085 ≈ roughly 54,938,000,000. So (3/2)^61 ≈ 5.4938e10. Then 2^97 = (2^10)^9 * 2^7 = 1024^9 * 128. 1024^2 is about a million, 1024^3 is about a billion, 1024^4 is a trillion, but 1024^10 is 1 yobibyte, but here it's 2^97 = 2^(10*9 + 7) = (2^10)^9 * 2^7 ≈ 1024^9 * 128. 1024^3 is 1,073,741,824. So 1024^9 = (1024^3)^3 = (1.073741824e9)^3 ≈ 1.237e27. Then multiplied by 128 gives approximately 1.237e27 * 128 ≈ 1.583e29. So 2^97 ≈ 1.583e29, whereas (3/2)^61 ≈ 5.49e10. Then 3^61 = (3/2)^61 * 2^61. Since 2^61 ≈ 2.305843009213694e18. Therefore, 3^61 ≈ 5.49e10 * 2.3058e18 ≈ 1.266e29. Comparing 1.266e29 (3^61) and 1.583e29 (2^97), so 3^61 is indeed smaller. Therefore, the minimum is 3^61.

But this is a very rough estimation. However, even with these approximations, it's clear that 3^61 is smaller. All methods point to the same conclusion. Therefore, I'm confident that 3^61 is the smaller number.

[duplicate]

Possible Duplicate:
Why are we using i as a counter in loops

I know this might seem like an absolutely silly question to ask, yet I am too curious not to ask...

Why did "i" and "j" become THE variables to use as counters in most control structures?

Although common sense tells me they are just like X, which is used for representing unknown values, I can't help to think that there must be a reason why everyone gets taught the same way over and over again.

Is it because it is actually recommended for best practices, or a convention, or does it have some obscure reason behind it?

Just in case, I know I can give them whatever name I want and that variables names are not relevant.

-
